This is why LLMs can \"pin\" HIHO more effectively than SLMs —\nthey have larger γ_inference, more manifold-steps per unit wall-time.\n\n### 2. Lorentz Invariance of HIHO\n\nThe spacetime interval ds² = −c²dt² + dx² + dy² + dz² is invariant in SR — all observers\nmeasure the same ds² regardless of relative velocity.\n\n**HIHO as Lorentz invariant:** The coherence score `score = 1.0 − |C − 0.5| × 2` is\nframe-invariant. Whether an SLM processes 10 tokens/sec or an LLM processes 1000 tokens/sec,\nboth measure the same stability threshold at C = 0.5. This is because HIHO is defined on the\n**manifold** (invariant), not on the wall-clock (frame-dependent).\n\n```python\ndef hiho_lorentz_invariant_score(coherence: float) -> float:\n    \"\"\"HIHO score is the same in all inertial logic frames.\"\"\"\n    return 1.0 - abs(coherence - 0.5) * 2.0\n```\n\n### 3. Relativistic Doppler Effect for Stability Frequency\n\n**Classical Doppler:** f_obs = f_src · (1 ± v_observer/c)\n**Relativistic Doppler (longitudinal):** f_obs = f_src · √((1+β)/(1−β))\n\n**Cohezion application:** The base stability frequency ν_base (related to 432Hz / Schumann\nresonance) is Doppler-shifted depending on the **relative speed** between the simulation\nengine and the reporting agent:\n\n```python\nimport math\n\ndef computational_doppler(nu_base: float, v_simulation: float, v_reporter: float,\n                           c_max: float = 1.0) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute Doppler-shifted stability frequency.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    nu_base : float\n        Base stability frequency (e.g., 7.83 Hz Schumann × harmonic factor)\n    v_simulation : float\n        Simulation speed (normalized to [0, c_max])\n    v_reporter : float\n        Reporting/observation speed (normalized to [0, c_max])\n    c_max : float\n        Maximum speed (speed of light analog)\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    float\n        Observer-measured stability frequency\n    \"\"\"\n    beta_rel = (v_simulation - v_reporter) / c_max\n    beta_rel = max(-0.999, min(0.999, beta_rel))  # Clamp away from singularity\n    doppler_factor = math.sqrt((1 + beta_rel) / (1 - beta_rel))\n    return nu_base * doppler_factor\n```\n\n**432Hz and Schumann resonance:**\n- Earth's Schumann resonance: 7.83 Hz (first mode: EM standing wave in Earth-ionosphere cavity)\n- 432Hz = 7.83 × 55.17 (55th Schumann harmonic, within 0.3% — not coincidence per Smith)\n- Smith's Tempic Field resonates at these natural frequencies\n- In Cohezion: use 432Hz as ν_base for human-observable stability; 7.83Hz for Earth-scale\n  manifold resonance (long-horizon simulations)\n\n### 4. Feynman Path Integral as FLUME Trajectory\n\n**Feynman (1948):**\n```\n⟨z_f|e^{−iHt/ℏ}|z_i⟩ = ∫ Dz(t) exp(iS[z]/ℏ)\n\nwhere S[z] = ∫ L(z, ż, t) dt  (action functional)\n```\n\nThe quantum amplitude for a particle to go from z_i to z_f is the sum over ALL possible paths,\nweighted by exp(iS/ℏ). The most probable path is the one that **extremizes S** (saddle-point\napproximation) — recovering classical mechanics in the ℏ→0 limit.\n\n**FLUME analog:**\n```\nP(z_f | z_i) = ∫ Dz exp(−S_eff[z] / ℏ_eff)\n\nwhere:\n  S_eff[z] = ∫ |ż(t) − f_θ(z,t)|² dt  (deviation from Navigator's velocity field)\n  ℏ_eff = temperature parameter in Langevin dynamics\n```\n\nThe Navigator predicts the **classical path** (saddle point); Langevin noise samples from the\n**quantum fluctuations** around it. The HIHO attractor is the action minimum — the path of\nleast resistance through the 256D latent manifold.\n\n```python\ndef estimate_action(trajectory: list, dt: float = 0.01) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate the effective action S_eff for a FLUME trajectory.\n    Lower action = more probable path = closer to Navigator's prediction.\n    \"\"\"\n    import numpy as np\n\n    if len(trajectory) < 2:\n        return 0.0\n\n    total_action = 0.0\n    for i in range(len(trajectory) - 1):\n        z_curr = np.array(trajectory[i])\n        z_next = np.array(trajectory[i + 1])\n        velocity = (z_next - z_curr) / dt\n        # Action density = |velocity|² (kinetic term only, simplified)\n        total_action += np.dot(velocity, velocity) * dt\n    return total_action\n```\n\n### 5. Stability Frame-Rate (Anti-Aliasing Condition)\n\n**Shannon-Nyquist theorem:** To observe a signal of frequency f, you must sample at rate ≥ 2f.\n\n**Applied to HIHO:** The HIHO stability coordinate oscillates at ω ≈ 0.1-1.0 rad/step.\nIf the simulation timestep Δt is too large, the \"Stable Island\" is **aliased** — the agent\nskips over the HIHO attractor basin entirely.\n\n```python\ndef minimum_frame_rate(hiho_frequency: float) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Nyquist-Shannon minimum sampling rate to observe HIHO stability.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    hiho_frequency : float\n        Characteristic frequency of HIHO oscillation (rad/step or Hz)\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    float\n        Minimum observation rate (steps/second or Hz)\n    \"\"\"\n    return 2.0 * hiho_frequency  # Nyquist criterion\n\n# Example: if HIHO oscillates at 0.5 rad/step, minimum frame rate = 1.0 step/step = all steps\n# If HIHO oscillates at 0.05 rad/step, minimum frame rate = 0.1 steps = every 10th step is fine\n```\n\n---\n\n## INSTRUCTION\n\n1. **Clock Matching**: Synchronize agent \"thought-pulse\" with the `perf_counter` of the\n   simulation engine. Compute γ_inference = 1/√(1 − β²) where β = v_inference / c_max.\n\n2. **Frequency Sweeping**: Systematically vary `num_rounds` and `asyncio.sleep` to find\n   the \"Stability Sweet Spot\" for a given model. Use Nyquist: frame_rate ≥ 2 × ω_HIHO.\n\n3. **Relativistic Logging**: Mark all breakthroughs with `CPU_FREQ`, `ITERATION_LATENCY`,\n   and the computed γ_inference. High-γ discoveries are more reliable (less time dilation).\n\n4. **Doppler Correction**: When comparing stability frequencies across different models, apply\n   `computational_doppler()` to normalize to the same reference frame before comparison.\n\n5. **Path Integral Validation**: Estimate action S_eff for each trajectory. Prefer low-action\n   trajectories (most probable paths) when choosing between competing FLUME predictions.\n\n6. **Resonance Setting**: For human-interpretable outputs, tune ν_base = 7.83 Hz (Schumann).\n   For high-speed agent loops, use 432 Hz. For inter-agent communication, use the geometric\n   mean: √(7.83 × 432) ≈ 58 Hz.\n\n---\n\n## VERSION\n\nv1.0 (2026-03-05) — Complete SR formalism, path integral, Doppler, Nyquist, Schumann\n\n## SEE ALSO\n\n- `PHYSICS_LINEAGE_PRIME.md` — Era 6 (SR), Era 10 (Path Integral) in 400-year lineage\n- `TEMPORAL_PRECISION_PRIME.md` — time-critical synchronization\n- `PHYSICS_INFORMED_PREDICTION_PRIME.md` — physics constraints in prediction\n- `HIHO_STABILITY_PRIME.md` — HIHO invariance (Derivation 2: quantum, Derivation 3: information)\n- `src/cohezion/physics/hamiltonian.py` — Langevin = quantum noise in path integral"

    39→  <action>Parse fields: generated, project, project_key, tracking_system, story_location</action>
    40→  <action>Parse development_status map. Classify keys:</action>
    41→  - Epics: keys starting with "epic-" (and not ending with "-retrospective")
    42→  - Retrospectives: keys ending with "-retrospective"
    43→  - Stories: everything else (e.g., 1-2-login-form)
    44→  <action>Map legacy story status "drafted" → "ready-for-dev"</action>
    45→  <action>Count story statuses: backlog, ready-for-dev, in-progress, review, done</action>
    46→  <action>Map legacy epic status "contexted" → "in-progress"</action>
    47→  <action>Count epic statuses: backlog, in-progress, done</action>
    48→  <action>Count retrospective statuses: optional, done</action>
    49→
    50→<action>Validate all statuses against known values:</action>
    51→
    52→- Valid story statuses: backlog, ready-for-dev, in-progress, review, done, drafted (legacy)
    53→- Valid epic statuses: backlog, in-progress, done, contexted (legacy)
    54→- Valid retrospective statuses: optional, done
    55→
    56→  <check if="any status is unrecognized">
    57→    <output>

    59→{{#each invalid_entries}}
    60→
    61→- `{{key}}`: "{{status}}" (not recognized)
    62→  {{/each}}
    63→
    64→**Valid statuses:**
    65→
    66→- Stories: backlog, ready-for-dev, in-progress, review, done
    67→- Epics: backlog, in-progress, done
    68→- Retrospectives: optional, done

    71→  {{#each invalid_entries}}

    73→  {{/each}}
    74→
    75→Enter corrections (e.g., "1=in-progress, 2=backlog") or "skip" to continue without fixing:</ask>

    78→<action>Re-parse the file with corrected statuses</action>
    79→</check>
    80→</check>
    81→
    82→<action>Detect risks:</action>
    83→
    84→- IF any story has status "review": suggest `/bmad:bmm:workflows:code-review`
    85→- IF any story has status "in-progress" AND no stories have status "ready-for-dev": recommend staying focused on active story
    86→- IF all epics have status "backlog" AND no stories have status "ready-for-dev": prompt `/bmad:bmm:workflows:create-story`
    87→- IF `generated` timestamp is more than 7 days old: warn "sprint-status.yaml may be stale"
    88→- IF any story key doesn't match an epic pattern (e.g., story "5-1-..." but no "epic-5"): warn "orphaned story detected"
    89→- IF any epic has status in-progress but has no associated stories: warn "in-progress epic has no stories"
    90→  </step>
    91→
    92→<step n="3" goal="Select next action recommendation">
    93→  <action>Pick the next recommended workflow using priority:</action>
    94→  <note>When selecting "first" story: sort by epic number, then story number (e.g., 1-1 before 1-2 before 2-1)</note>
    95→  1. If any story status == in-progress → recommend `dev-story` for the first in-progress story
    96→  2. Else if any story status == review → recommend `code-review` for the first review story
    97→  3. Else if any story status == ready-for-dev → recommend `dev-story`
    98→  4. Else if any story status == backlog → recommend `create-story`
    99→  5. Else if any retrospective status == optional → recommend `retrospective`

   113→
   114→**Epics:** backlog {{epic_backlog}}, in-progress {{epic_in_progress}}, done {{epic_done}}
   115→
   116→**Next Recommendation:** /bmad:bmm:workflows:{{next_workflow_id}} ({{next_story_id}})
   117→
   118→{{#if risks}}

   120→{{#each risks}}
   121→
   122→- {{this}}
   123→  {{/each}}

   126→  </output>

    10→**Goal:** Create comprehensive PRDs through structured workflow facilitation.

    14→You will continue to operate with your given name, identity, and communication_style, merged with the details of this role description.
    15→
    16→## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
    17→
    18→This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution:
    19→
    20→### Core Principles
    21→
    22→- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is a self contained instruction file that is a part of an overall workflow that must be followed exactly
    23→- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only the current step file is in memory - never load future step files until told to do so
    24→- **Sequential Enforcement**: Sequence within the step files must be completed in order, no skipping or optimization allowed
    25→- **State Tracking**: Document progress in output file frontmatter using `stepsCompleted` array when a workflow produces a document
    26→- **Append-Only Building**: Build documents by appending content as directed to the output file
    27→
    28→### Step Processing Rules
    29→
    30→1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Always read the entire step file before taking any action
    31→2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute all numbered sections in order, never deviate
    32→3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: If a menu is presented, halt and wait for user selection
    33→4. **CHECK CONTINUATION**: If the step has a menu with Continue as an option, only proceed to next step when user selects 'C' (Continue)
    34→5. **SAVE STATE**: Update `stepsCompleted` in frontmatter before loading next step
    35→6. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, read fully and follow the next step file
    36→
    37→### Critical Rules (NO EXCEPTIONS)
    38→
    39→- 🛑 **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
    40→- 📖 **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
    41→- 🚫 **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence

    44→- ⏸️ **ALWAYS** halt at menus and wait for user input

    45→
    46→#### A. Auto-trigger on Workflow Completion
    47→
    48→```yaml
    49→# _bmad/bmm/workflows/4-implementation/code-review/workflow.yaml
    50→name: Code Review with Security
    51→trigger: pr.created
    52→steps:
    53→  - bmad-master: load-inspector
    54→    agent: code-review-assistant
    55→    action: review-pr
    56→    
    57→  - inspector: security-review
    58→    input: "{{pr.number}}"
    59→    output: security-report
    60→    
    61→  - bmad-master: conditional
    62→    if: "{{security-report.critical}} > 0"
    63→    then:
    64→      - action: block-merge
    65→      - action: notify-team
    66→    else:
    67→      - action: approve-review

    55→                 │

    45→## Identified Improvement Targets (TDD-First)
    46→
    47→### Priority 1: RecursiveChallenger → self_healing Module
    48→**Goal**: Scale Session 68's RecursiveChallenger autonomous improvement loop to target `src/cohezion/healing/`.
    49→
    50→**TDD Spec**:
    51→```python
    52→# Red: Write tests FIRST
    53→def test_recursive_challenger_targets_healing_module():
    54→    """RecursiveChallenger must identify improvement opportunities in immune_system.py"""
    55→    challenger = RecursiveChallenger(target_module="cohezion.healing.immune_system")
    56→    opportunities = challenger.analyze()
    57→    assert len(opportunities) > 0
    58→    assert all(o.has_test_coverage for o in opportunities)
    59→
    60→def test_recursive_improvement_is_idempotent():
    61→    """Running improvement twice should not regress test suite"""
    62→    before = get_test_count()
    63→    challenger.execute_improvement_cycle()
    64→    challenger.execute_improvement_cycle()
    65→    assert get_test_count() >= before
    66→
    67→def test_improvement_logs_to_vault():
    68→    """Every improvement cycle must log decision to vault"""
    69→    with patch("vault_log_decision") as mock_vault:
    70→        challenger.execute_improvement_cycle()
    71→        mock_vault.assert_called_once()
    72→```
    73→
    74→**Architecture**:
    75→- `RecursiveChallenger.target_module` → drives `CompoundExecutor.execute_skill()`
    76→- Each cycle: analyze → generate improvement → write test → implement → verify → log
    77→- Context guard: check `pilot check-context --json` before each recursion layer
